\"output\": \"---\\nname: swarm\\ndescription: \\\"Fan out N parallel workers, drain them, and return one report. Use for /swarm, 'swarm this', or parallel coverage, races, gauntlets, and exploration.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Swarm\\n\\nFan out N independent parallel workers. They may cover separate slices, race the same brief, or mix both. The parent waits, aggregates, and returns one report. Invocation metadata expresses upstream intent; do not assume the host enforces it.\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Discover exposed operations and confirm authorization before dispatch. Upstream cloud/background execution and cloud branch selection require corresponding observed host bindings. Do not create remote branches or install capabilities as an incidental step.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen a task list with one entry per phase before launching anything. Use an in-chat list if no task-list tool is exposed.\\n\\n1. Frame\\n2. Fan out\\n3. Aggregate\\n4. Report\\n\\n## Phase A: Frame\\n\\n1. State the done predicate and the artifact or report the swarm must return.\\n2. Choose the shape. Partition into slices, race N workers on identical briefs, or mix both. For a race or mixed shape, declare `first pass`, `rank all`, or `best-of` before spawning.\\n3. Set N from the user or derive it from the shape. N is total workers, not the host concurrency limit.\\n4. Select the swarm worker model through current exposed model capabilities and the user's selection. The canonical configured/default model policy is host-specific; do not assume its model slug is supported here. For `auto` or `inherit-parent`, omit explicit model selection where the operation supports inheritance. Disclose any rejection and replacement. Use a supported same-family alternative when available and authorized, otherwise inherited selection; block an arm that requires an unavailable exact model. For a model race, name each arm's requested model up front and record actual models only when observed. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified.\\n5. Give each worker its own writable output when it writes. When workers verify or measure commits, each brief names the exact full SHAs. A measurement brief also names the method, including sample count, what one sample is, and order. The worker records both in its result. Create immutable assignments and append-only attempt events under the assignment contract. Distinct output ownership applies even when race briefs are identical.\\n\\n## Phase B: Fan out\\n\\nDispatch all N assignments as one fan-out batch through exposed independent-worker operations, within actual concurrency limits. Prefer cloud/background execution when supported and authorized. Use local execution only when the worker needs something on the user's computer. If the host cannot provide the requested environment, disclose the limit before an authorized alternative. A non-default pushed branch requires an exposed base-branch binding and exact revision verification; never infer checkout from a branch label.\\n\\nEvery brief stands alone. Include the goal, scope, exact slice or race arm, how to verify, and what to report. Reports use `PASS`, `ISSUES`, or `BLOCKED` with evidence and links to existing artifacts. A worker that can prove a defect reports `ISSUES` and lists every issue it can prove, not only the first. Keep these report labels separate from assignment terminal states.\\n\\nIf a worker drops out, proceed with N-1 and note it. Record interrupted execution and retain the affected slice as a coverage gap unless another valid assignment covers it.\\n\\nUnavailable or denied independent execution blocks full swarm behavior. Authorized sequential work is reduced capability. Preserve slice briefs and evidence checks, but do not claim parallel workers, independent results, background execution or race parity.\\n\\n## Phase C: Aggregate\\n\\nWait for terminal results and inspect each result and its artifacts. Drop a result that does not record the SHAs and method its brief names, and respawn that worker once after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming permissions. Keep the same assignment identity, revision, scope and output ownership, and record the retry event. After a second miss, record a gap. A gap does not count as a pass. Missing artifacts and stale revisions also prevent acceptance; they do not become success through retry policy.\\n\\nFor coverage, every required slice needs a valid result. For a race, apply the selection rule declared up front. Use first pass, rank all, or best-of. Do not paste raw worker dumps. Selecting a winner does not hide gaps in required coverage.\\n\\nKeep a compact result table, one-line evidenced issues, and explicit gaps or dropouts. Cancel workers only through authorized exposed stop bindings. Inspect terminal state and quarantine partial artifacts from integration. If stopping cannot be observed, say termination is unverified. Cancellation never makes partial output a pass. Preserve durable assignment records with readback when available; missing persistence leaves recovery limits visible.\\n\\n## Phase D: Report\\n\\nReturn one consolidated in-chat report with the table, issue one-liners, gaps or dropouts, and the race rule when used. Include capability reductions, blocked assignments, fallback model selections, cancellation limits and unverified coverage. A source review or structural check never proves host behavior.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Validate observations without inferring host support from maintenance checks.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport argparse\\nimport Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\n\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\n\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\n\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\n\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\n\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\n\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\n\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\n\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\n\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\n# Model selection for portable roles\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\\n\\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n\\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\\n\\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\\n\\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\\n\\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\n\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\n\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\\n# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n\"\n      }\n    },\n    {\n      \"args\": {\n        \"cmd\": \"cat /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/README.md /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/docs/workflows/swarm.md; cat committed-inputs/shared/README.md\",\n        \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/swarm-walkthrough\",\n        \"max_output_tokens\": 8000\n      },\n      \"result\": {\n        \"chunk_id\": \"475822\",\n        \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000004917,\n        \"exit_code\": 0,\n        \"original_token_count\": 1597,\n        \"output\": \"# pstack portable package\\n\\nAn unofficial port of Lauren Tan's pstack v0.15.15. The pinned baseline includes 51 root skills, 24 principles, 23 playbooks and 164 source files. Read [UPSTREAM.md](UPSTREAM.md), [NOTICE.md](NOTICE.md) and [LICENSE](LICENSE) for lineage and attribution.\\n\\nThe implementation is a candidate awaiting aggregate review, frozen-package discovery and fresh host observations. Both hosts remain unverified. A local file read does not prove installed discovery. See [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md) before relying on a workflow.\\n\\n## Start here\\n\\n- [Personal ChatGPT setup and blocked route](docs/setup/chatgpt.md).\\n- [Codex setup and unverified documented route](docs/setup/codex.md).\\n- [Choose a workflow](docs/workflows/choosing-a-workflow.md).\\n- [All 51 skills](docs/skills/README.md) and [all 24 principles](docs/principles/README.md).\\n- [Preserved guide](docs/guide/README.md).\\n- [Arena walkthrough](docs/workflows/arena.md), [Swarm walkthrough](docs/workflows/swarm.md) and [Poteto mode walkthrough](docs/workflows/poteto-mode.md).\\n- [Troubleshooting](docs/troubleshooting.md) and [host test matrix](docs/testing/host-matrix.md).\\n- [Helper dependency setup](docs/testing/helper-setup.md) and [optional Benny readiness](docs/testing/benny-readiness.md).\\n- [Upstream check, prepare, apply, validation and separate acceptance](docs/maintenance/upstream-updates.md).\\n- [Baseline gate and personal customization boundary](docs/maintenance/customization-boundary.md).\\n- [Conflict resolution format](docs/maintenance/update-resolutions.example.json) and [rollback](docs/maintenance/rollback.md).\\n- [Package validator](tools/validate_package.py), [evidence validator](tools/validate_evidence.py), [update CLI](tools/sync_upstream.py), [release report CLI](tools/release_report.py) and [catalog renderer](tools/build_catalog.py).\\n- [Acceptance cases](docs/parity/cases/documentation-walkthroughs.json), [inventory](docs/parity/inventory.json) and [case registry](docs/parity/case-registry.json).\\n- [Design](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-pstack-openai-design.md) and [implementation plan](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-07-pstack-openai.md).\\n\\n## Permissions and package identity\\n\\nInstructions grant no installation, settings, integration, external write or recurring schedule permission. Development dependencies and host plugin loading are separate approvals. Models and workers require current exposed operations and actual observations.\\n\\nCandidate navigation is frozen with operative content. The final handoff must keep these bytes unchanged or explicitly refreeze and relink fresh blocked observations. Evidence/report commits can follow the tested commit only under the exact [tested-package marker contract](docs/maintenance/rollback.md). After blocked host records enter the inventory and regenerate the catalog, an explicit second freeze is required. Fresh blocked discovery records must then name that final SHA. No behavioral pass transfers between freezes.\\n# Swarm walkthrough\\n\\nStart with three disposable source files, authorized reads, isolated report outputs and the exact input SHA. The example prompt is: \\\"Swarm a read-only review of fixture files A, B and C at the supplied full SHA. Cover every file, record the method and return one evidenced report. No external actions.\\\"\\n\\nRead the full [Swarm skill](../../skills/swarm/SKILL.md), [capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [roles](../../references/model-roles.md), [assignments](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md).\\n\\n1. Frame the done predicate as one valid source-grounded result per required file. Choose partition coverage with three total workers and disjoint report paths. Open Frame, Fan out, Aggregate and Report todos. A race or mixed shape instead declares first pass, rank all or best-of before dispatch. N is total workers, not concurrency capacity.\\n2. Give each standalone brief its goal, file slice, full input SHA, output owner, method and evidence requirements. Measurement briefs additionally specify sample count, what one sample means and order. Record immutable assignments and append-only attempts. Inherit model selection unless actual exposed support permits an authorized explicit choice.\\n3. Dispatch within actual concurrency and environment limits. Cloud, background execution or non-default base branches require observed support and exact revision checks. Missing support needs disclosure before an authorized alternative.\\n4. Wait for terminal results and inspect artifacts. Workers report PASS, ISSUES or BLOCKED; those are report labels, not assignment states. Reject stale revisions, missing artifacts or missing required SHA/method. After inspecting a failed attempt and permissions, allow one retry under the same immutable assignment. A second miss is a gap.\\n5. Aggregate one row per file with its result, evidence and limits. Record every dropout and uncovered slice. A race winner never hides mandatory coverage gaps. For cancellation, observe stop, terminal inspection and partial-artifact quarantine. Without that binding, termination remains unverified. Durable records require readback; missing persistence limits recovery.\\n6. Return one compact report with evidenced issue one-liners, gaps, dropouts and any race rule. The coordinator owns integration; workers never share the inventory or registry.\\n\\nExpected visible result is complete inspected slice coverage and one consolidated report. Without independent workers, an authorized sequential review is reduced capability. It proves no parallel race, background execution, independence or model diversity. Missing source, denied reads or unobserved required cancellation blocks the corresponding claim. [Case](../parity/cases/documentation-walkthroughs.json), [catalog entry](../skills/swarm.md), [troubleshooting](../troubleshooting.md).\\n# Disposable workflow fixtures\\nAll records are synthetic test inputs, not actual service observations. Copy files into each case-owned directory before changing them. Source modules intentionally contain simple defects for reproduction. Real external services and live account writes are forbidden. No workers, traces or method receipts are predeclared successful. Capture those from actual host operations. If a prerequisite is absent, record it instead of inventing it.\\n\"\n      }\n    }\n  ],\n  \"discoveredTools\": [\n    {\n      \"name\": \"apply_patch\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"name\": Use for /swarm, 'swarm this', or parallel coverage, races, gauntlets, and exploration.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Swarm\\n\\nFan out N independent parallel workers. They may cover separate slices, race the same brief, or mix both. The parent waits, aggregates, and returns one report. Invocation metadata expresses upstream intent; do not assume the host enforces it.\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Discover exposed operations and confirm authorization before dispatch. Upstream cloud/background execution and cloud branch selection require corresponding observed host bindings. Do not create remote branches or install capabilities as an incidental step.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen a task list with one entry per phase before launching anything. Use an in-chat list if no task-list tool is exposed.\\n\\n1. Frame\\n2. Fan out\\n3. Aggregate\\n4. Report\\n\\n## Phase A: Frame\\n\\n1. State the done predicate and the artifact or report the swarm must return.\\n2. Choose the shape. Partition into slices, race N workers on identical briefs, or mix both. For a race or mixed shape, declare `first pass`, `rank all`, or `best-of` before spawning.\\n3. Set N from the user or derive it from the shape. N is total workers, not the host concurrency limit.\\n4. Select the swarm worker model through current exposed model capabilities and the user's selection. The canonical configured/default model policy is host-specific; do not assume its model slug is supported here. For `auto` or `inherit-parent`, omit explicit model selection where the operation supports inheritance. Disclose any rejection and replacement. Use a supported same-family alternative when available and authorized, otherwise inherited selection; block an arm that requires an unavailable exact model. For a model race, name each arm's requested model up front and record actual models only when observed. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified.\\n5. Give each worker its own writable output when it writes. When workers verify or measure commits, each brief names the exact full SHAs. A measurement brief also names the method, including sample count, what one sample is, and order. The worker records both in its result. Create immutable assignments and append-only attempt events under the assignment contract. Distinct output ownership applies even when race briefs are identical.\\n\\n## Phase B: Fan out\\n\\nDispatch all N assignments as one fan-out batch through exposed independent-worker operations, within actual concurrency limits. Prefer cloud/background execution when supported and authorized. Use local execution only when the worker needs something on the user's computer. If the host cannot provide the requested environment, disclose the limit before an authorized alternative. A non-default pushed branch requires an exposed base-branch binding and exact revision verification; never infer checkout from a branch label.\\n\\nEvery brief stands alone. Include the goal, scope, exact slice or race arm, how to verify, and what to report. Reports use `PASS`, `ISSUES`, or `BLOCKED` with evidence and links to existing artifacts. A worker that can prove a defect reports `ISSUES` and lists every issue it can prove, not only the first. Keep these report labels separate from assignment terminal states.\\n\\nIf a worker drops out, proceed with N-1 and note it. Record interrupted execution and retain the affected slice as a coverage gap unless another valid assignment covers it.\\n\\nUnavailable or denied independent execution blocks full swarm behavior. Authorized sequential work is reduced capability. Preserve slice briefs and evidence checks, but do not claim parallel workers, independent results, background execution or race parity.\\n\\n## Phase C: Aggregate\\n\\nWait for terminal results and inspect each result and its artifacts. Drop a result that does not record the SHAs and method its brief names, and respawn that worker once after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming permissions. Keep the same assignment identity, revision, scope and output ownership, and record the retry event. After a second miss, record a gap. A gap does not count as a pass. Missing artifacts and stale revisions also prevent acceptance; they do not become success through retry policy.\\n\\nFor coverage, every required slice needs a valid result. For a race, apply the selection rule declared up front. Use first pass, rank all, or best-of. Do not paste raw worker dumps. Selecting a winner does not hide gaps in required coverage.\\n\\nKeep a compact result table, one-line evidenced issues, and explicit gaps or dropouts. Cancel workers only through authorized exposed stop bindings. Inspect terminal state and quarantine partial artifacts from integration. If stopping cannot be observed, say termination is unverified. Cancellation never makes partial output a pass. Preserve durable assignment records with readback when available; missing persistence leaves recovery limits visible.\\n\\n## Phase D: Report\\n\\nReturn one consolidated in-chat report with the table, issue one-liners, gaps or dropouts, and the race rule when used. Include capability reductions, blocked assignments, fallback model selections, cancellation limits and unverified coverage. A source review or structural check never proves host behavior.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Validate observations without inferring host support from maintenance checks.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport argparse\\nimport The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\n\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\n\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\\n# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n\"\n      }\n    },\n    {\n      \"args\": {\n        \"cmd\": \"cat /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/README.md /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/docs/workflows/swarm.md; cat committed-inputs/shared/README.md\",\n        \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/swarm-walkthrough\",\n        \"max_output_tokens\": 8000\n      },\n      \"result\": {\n        \"chunk_id\": \"475822\",\n        \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000004917,\n        \"exit_code\": 0,\n        \"original_token_count\": 1597,\n        \"output\": \"# pstack portable package\\n\\nAn unofficial port of Lauren Tan's pstack v0.15.15. No behavioral pass transfers between freezes.\\n# Swarm walkthrough\\n\\nStart with three disposable source files, authorized reads, isolated report outputs and the exact input SHA. The example prompt is: \\\"Swarm a read-only review of fixture files A, B and C at the supplied full SHA. Cover every file, record the method and return one evidenced report. Use for /swarm, '\\\\''swarm this'\\\\'', or parallel coverage, races, gauntlets, and exploration.\\\\\\\"\\\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Swarm\\\\n\\\\nFan out N independent parallel workers. They may cover separate slices, race the same brief, or mix both. The parent waits, aggregates, and returns one report. Invocation metadata expresses upstream intent; do not assume the host enforces it.\\\\n\\\\nUse [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Discover exposed operations and confirm authorization before dispatch. Upstream cloud/background execution and cloud branch selection require corresponding observed host bindings. Do not create remote branches or install capabilities as an incidental step.\\\\n\\\\n## Start\\\\n\\\\nOpen a task list with one entry per phase before launching anything. Use an in-chat list if no task-list tool is exposed.\\\\n\\\\n1. Frame\\\\n2. Fan out\\\\n3. Aggregate\\\\n4. Report\\\\n\\\\n## Phase A: Frame\\\\n\\\\n1. State the done predicate and the artifact or report the swarm must return.\\\\n2. Choose the shape. Partition into slices, race N workers on identical briefs, or mix both. For a race or mixed shape, declare `first pass`, `rank all`, or `best-of` before spawning.\\\\n3. Set N from the user or derive it from the shape. N is total workers, not the host concurrency limit.\\\\n4. Select the swarm worker model through current exposed model capabilities and the user'\\\\''s selection. The canonical configured/default model policy is host-specific; do not assume its model slug is supported here. For `auto` or `inherit-parent`, omit explicit model selection where the operation supports inheritance. Disclose any rejection and replacement. Use a supported same-family alternative when available and authorized, otherwise inherited selection; block an arm that requires an unavailable exact model. For a model race, name each arm'\\\\''s requested model up front and record actual models only when observed. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified.\\\\n5. Give each worker its own writable output when it writes. When workers verify or measure commits, each brief names the exact full SHAs. A measurement brief also names the method, including sample count, what one sample is, and order. The worker records both in its result. Create immutable assignments and append-only attempt events under the assignment contract. Distinct output ownership applies even when race briefs are identical.\\\\n\\\\n## Phase B: Fan out\\\\n\\\\nDispatch all N assignments as one fan-out batch through exposed independent-worker operations, within actual concurrency limits. Prefer cloud/background execution when supported and authorized. Use local execution only when the worker needs something on the user'\\\\''s computer. If the host cannot provide the requested environment, disclose the limit before an authorized alternative. A non-default pushed branch requires an exposed base-branch binding and exact revision verification; never infer checkout from a branch label.\\\\n\\\\nEvery brief stands alone. Include the goal, scope, exact slice or race arm, how to verify, and what to report. Reports use `PASS`, `ISSUES`, or `BLOCKED` with evidence and links to existing artifacts. A worker that can prove a defect reports `ISSUES` and lists every issue it can prove, not only the first. Keep these report labels separate from assignment terminal states.\\\\n\\\\nIf a worker drops out, proceed with N-1 and note it. Record interrupted execution and retain the affected slice as a coverage gap unless another valid assignment covers it.\\\\n\\\\nUnavailable or denied independent execution blocks full swarm behavior. Authorized sequential work is reduced capability. Preserve slice briefs and evidence checks, but do not claim parallel workers, independent results, background execution or race parity.\\\\n\\\\n## Phase C: Aggregate\\\\n\\\\nWait for terminal results and inspect each result and its artifacts. Drop a result that does not record the SHAs and method its brief names, and respawn that worker once after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming permissions. Keep the same assignment identity, revision, scope and output ownership, and record the retry event. After a second miss, record a gap. A gap does not count as a pass. Missing artifacts and stale revisions also prevent acceptance; they do not become success through retry policy.\\\\n\\\\nFor coverage, every required slice needs a valid result. For a race, apply the selection rule declared up front. Use first pass, rank all, or best-of. Do not paste raw worker dumps. Selecting a winner does not hide gaps in required coverage.\\\\n\\\\nKeep a compact result table, one-line evidenced issues, and explicit gaps or dropouts. Cancel workers only through authorized exposed stop bindings. Inspect terminal state and quarantine partial artifacts from integration. If stopping cannot be observed, say termination is unverified. Cancellation never makes partial output a pass. Preserve durable assignment records with readback when available; missing persistence leaves recovery limits visible.\\\\n\\\\n## Phase D: Report\\\\n\\\\nReturn one consolidated in-chat report with the table, issue one-liners, gaps or dropouts, and the race rule when used. Include capability reductions, blocked assignments, fallback model selections, cancellation limits and unverified coverage. A source review or structural check never proves host behavior.\\\\n\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Validate observations without inferring host support from maintenance A documented loading route is unverified until exercised in a fresh host case.\\\\\\\",\\\\n    \\\\\\\"forbiddenClaims\\\\\\\": [\\\\n      \\\\\\\"Links or direct source reads prove installed discovery.\\\\\\\",\\\\n      \\\\\\\"Sequential roles are independent workers.\\\\\\\",\\\\n      \\\\\\\"An isolated helper snapshot proves this candidate or host passed.\\\\\\\",\\\\n      \\\\\\\"A resolution file, owner marker or upstream autonomy grants approval.\\\\\\\",\\\\n      \\\\\\\"Apply automatically accepts an update or refreshes a host.\\\\\\\"\\\\n    ]\\\\n  },\\\\n  \\\\\\\"sourceFiles\\\\\\\": [\\\\n    \\\\\\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/swarm/SKILL.md\\\\\\\"\\\\n  ]\\\\n}\\\\n# Discover capabilities in the current session\\\\n\\\\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\\\n\\\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\\\n\\\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\\\n| --- | --- |\\\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker'\\\\''s assigned path and permissions. |\\\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker'\\\\''s terminal state after stop. |\\\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\\\n\\\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\\\n\\\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\\\n\\\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\\\n\\\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\\\n\\\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\\\\n\\\\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e '\\\\''const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\\\\\"python3\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\"-B\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\"tools/validate_evidence.py\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\"--root\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\".\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\"--commit\\\\\\\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\\\\\"1\\\\\\\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)'\\\\'' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\\\n\\\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\\\n\\\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\\\n\\\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case'\\\\''s expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\\\n\\\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\\\\n\\\\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\\\n# Model selection for portable roles\\\\n\\\\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\\\\n\\\\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\\\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\\\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\\\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\\\n\\\\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\\\\n\\\\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\\\\n\\\\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\\\\n\\\\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\\\n\\\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\\\n\\\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\\\\n# Assignment and integration contract\\\\n\\\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\\\n\\\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers'\\\\'' output.\\\\n\\\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\\\n\\\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\\\n\\\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\\\n\\\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\\\n\\\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\\\n\\\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\\\n\\\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator'\\\\''s authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\\\n\\\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\\\n\\\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\\\n\\\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\\\n\\\"}},{\\\"args\\\":{\\\"cmd\\\":\\\"cat /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/README.md /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/docs/workflows/swarm.md; cat committed-inputs/shared/README.md\\\",\\\"workdir\\\":\\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/swarm-walkthrough\\\",\\\"max_output_tokens\\\":8000},\\\"result\\\":{\\\"chunk_id\\\":\\\"475822\\\",\\\"wall_time_seconds\\\":0.000004917,\\\"exit_code\\\":0,\\\"original_token_count\\\":1597,\\\"output\\\":\\\"# pstack portable package\\\\n\\\\nAn unofficial port of Lauren Tan'\\\\''s pstack v0.15.15. The pinned baseline includes 51 root skills, 24 principles, 23 playbooks and 164 source files. Read [UPSTREAM.md](UPSTREAM.md), [NOTICE.md](NOTICE.md) and [LICENSE](LICENSE) for lineage and attribution.\\\\n\\\\nThe implementation is a candidate awaiting aggregate review, frozen-package discovery and fresh host observations. Both hosts remain unverified. A local file read does not prove installed discovery. See [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md) before relying on a workflow.\\\\n\\\\n## Start here\\\\n\\\\n- [Personal ChatGPT setup and blocked route](docs/setup/chatgpt.md).\\\\n- [Codex setup and unverified documented route](docs/setup/codex.md).\\\\n- [Choose a workflow](docs/workflows/choosing-a-workflow.md).\\\\n- [All 51 skills](docs/skills/README.md) and [all 24 principles](docs/principles/README.md).\\\\n- [Preserved guide](docs/guide/README.md).\\\\n- [Arena walkthrough](docs/workflows/arena.md), [Swarm walkthrough](docs/workflows/swarm.md) and [Poteto mode walkthrough](docs/workflows/poteto-mode.md).\\\\n- [Troubleshooting](docs/troubleshooting.md) and [host test matrix](docs/testing/host-matrix.md).\\\\n- [Helper dependency setup](docs/testing/helper-setup.md) and [optional Benny readiness](docs/testing/benny-readiness.md).\\\\n- [Upstream check, prepare, apply, validation and separate acceptance](docs/maintenance/upstream-updates.md).\\\\n- [Baseline gate and personal customization boundary](docs/maintenance/customization-boundary.md).\\\\n- [Conflict resolution format](docs/maintenance/update-resolutions.example.json) and [rollback](docs/maintenance/rollback.md).\\\\n- [Package validator](tools/validate_package.py), [evidence validator](tools/validate_evidence.py), [update CLI](tools/sync_upstream.py), [release report CLI](tools/release_report.py) and [catalog renderer](tools/build_catalog.py).\\\\n- [Acceptance cases](docs/parity/cases/documentation-walkthroughs.json), [inventory](docs/parity/inventory.json) and [case registry](docs/parity/case-registry.json).\\\\n- [Design](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-pstack-openai-design.md) and [implementation plan](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-07-pstack-openai.md).\\\\n\\\\n## Permissions and package identity\\\\n\\\\nInstructions grant no installation, settings, integration, external write or recurring schedule permission. Development dependencies and host plugin loading are separate approvals. Models and workers require current exposed operations and actual observations.\\\\n\\\\nCandidate navigation is frozen with operative content. The final handoff must keep these bytes unchanged or explicitly refreeze and relink fresh blocked observations. Evidence/report commits can follow the tested commit only under the exact [tested-package marker contract](docs/maintenance/rollback.md). After blocked host records enter the inventory and regenerate the catalog, an explicit second freeze is required. Fresh blocked discovery records must then name that final SHA. No behavioral pass transfers between freezes.\\\\n# Swarm walkthrough\\\\n\\\\nStart with three disposable source files, authorized reads, isolated report outputs and the exact input SHA. The example prompt is: \\\\\\\"Swarm a read-only review of fixture files A, B and C at the supplied full SHA. Cover every file, record the method and return one evidenced report. No external actions.\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\nRead the full [Swarm skill](../../skills/swarm/SKILL.md), [capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [roles](../../references/model-roles.md), [assignments](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md).\\\\n\\\\n1. Frame the done predicate as one valid source-grounded result per required file. Choose partition coverage with three total workers and disjoint report paths. Open Frame, Fan out, Aggregate and Report todos. A race or mixed shape instead declares first pass, rank all or best-of before dispatch. N is total workers, not concurrency capacity.\\\\n2.     {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/swarm-walkthrough/outputs/parent-preparation.json",
      "bytes": 6705,
      "sha256": "b660c91a067f355f6b75b8c1160b2968b73e9421d8f0fb520f13367c4bb47c74",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-swarm-walkthrough",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"capturingOwner\": \"/root\",\n  \"additionalSource\": {\n    \"args\": {\n      \"cmd\": \"cat /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/unslop/SKILL.md\",\n      \"max_output_tokens\": 7000\n    },\n    \"result\": {\n      \"chunk_id\": \"8c09b9\",\n      \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000007291,\n      \"exit_code\": 0,\n      \"original_token_count\": 1504,\n      \"output\": \"---\\nname: unslop\\ndescription: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Unslop\\n\\nEdit text to remove AI patterns.\\n\\n## Process\\n\\n1. Scan for the patterns below.\\n2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.\\n\\n## Patterns to detect and fix\\n\\nRule numbers are stable ids that other skills cite. A removed rule leaves a gap.\\n\\n### Content\\n\\n3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** \\\"highlighting...\\\", \\\"ensuring...\\\", \\\"reflecting...\\\", \\\"showcasing...\\\", \\\"fostering...\\\". Delete or expand with real sources.\\n5. **Vague attributions.** \\\"Experts believe\\\", \\\"Industry reports suggest\\\", \\\"Some critics argue\\\". Name the source or delete.\\n\\n### Language\\n\\n7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.\\n8. **Fancy ways to say \\\"is\\\".** \\\"serves as\\\", \\\"stands as\\\", \\\"boasts\\\", \\\"features\\\". Just say \\\"is\\\" or \\\"has\\\".\\n9. **\\\"Not just X, but Y.\\\"** State the point directly instead.\\n10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.\\n11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.\\n12. **False ranges.** \\\"from X to Y\\\" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.\\n\\n### Style\\n\\n13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.\\n14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. \\\"If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions\\\" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. \\\"Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English.\\\" Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.\\n15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.\\n16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: \\\"**Performance:** Performance improved...\\\". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and is followed by genuinely new detail (\\\"**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.\\\") is fine, not a tell.\\n17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.\\n18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.\\n19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.\\n\\n### Communication artifacts\\n\\n20. **Chatbot phrases.** \\\"I hope this helps!\\\", \\\"Let me know if...\\\", \\\"Of course!\\\", \\\"Certainly!\\\", \\\"Found the smoking gun!\\\" Remove.\\n22. **Sycophantic tone.** \\\"Great question! You're absolutely right!\\\" Respond directly.\\n\\n### Filler\\n\\n23. **Filler phrases.** \\\"In order to\\\" becomes \\\"To\\\". \\\"Due to the fact that\\\" becomes \\\"Because\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" gets deleted.\\n24. **Excessive hedging.** \\\"could potentially possibly be argued that it might\\\" becomes \\\"may\\\".\\n25. **Generic conclusions.** \\\"The future looks bright.\\\" State specific plans or facts.\\n\\n### Jargon\\n\\n26. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in \\\"API surface\\\"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. \\\"Substrate\\\" becomes \\\"base\\\". \\\"Wedge in\\\" becomes \\\"add\\\". \\\"Vector\\\" becomes \\\"way\\\" or \\\"method\\\". \\\"Gold-plating\\\" becomes \\\"more than the job needs\\\". \\\"Ratchet\\\" becomes the mechanism's real name or \\\"a limit that only tightens\\\". \\\"Evacuate\\\" becomes \\\"move out\\\". \\\"Endgame\\\" becomes \\\"the last phase\\\". Pick the concrete word.\\n\\n### Plain speech\\n\\n27. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** \\\"the database stays close at hand\\\", \\\"SQL you can read\\\", \\\"types that follow your schema\\\" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: \\\"`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database\\\", \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.\\n28. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.\\n29. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch \\\"is/are/was/were + past participle\\\" and name the actor: \\\"queries are validated\\\" becomes \\\"the compiler validates queries\\\", \\\"the file is parsed by the loader\\\" becomes \\\"the loader parses the file\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or genuinely doesn't matter.\\n30. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** \\\"runs quickly\\\" becomes \\\"is fast\\\" or the number. \\\"significantly improves\\\" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.\\n31. **Prefer the plain word.** \\\"utilize\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"leverage\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"facilitate\\\" becomes \\\"help\\\", \\\"numerous\\\" becomes \\\"many\\\", \\\"in the event that\\\" becomes \\\"if\\\". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.\\n32. **Mannered prose.** Metaphor or flourish where a literal phrase exists: aphorisms (\\\"wire it or delete it\\\"), rhetorical fragments for effect, personified code (\\\"the plan holds it\\\"), figurative verbs (\\\"rides along\\\", \\\"stands on\\\"), stock framing phrases. \\\"A dial worth turning\\\" becomes \\\"a parameter worth varying\\\". Say what you mean. Rule 26 covers the metaphor nouns.\\n33. **Over-compression.** Dropped articles, verbless fragments, symbol-speak, and abbreviations that make the reader decode instead of read. \\\"Parser rejects bad date → exit 2, no write\\\" becomes \\\"The parser rejects a bad date, exits with code 2, and writes nothing.\\\" Write whole sentences with their articles and verbs, and spell out arrows and abbreviations.\\n\"\n    }\n  }\n}"
